Weiterleiten\" ist die bevorzugte\n66: aufgerufene Methode, da sie Sicherheitsvorkehrungen enthält, die sicherstellen, dass alles auf dem erwarteten Gerät funktioniert. The problem is that in the EsmTokenizer class, the `_add_tokens` method is overridden to force `special_tokens=True`:\n\n```python\ndef _add_tokens(self, new_tokens: Union[List[str], List[AddedToken]], special_tokens: bool = False) -> int:\n    return super()._add_tokens(new_tokens, special_tokens=True)\n```\n\nThis is hardcoding `special_tokens=True` regardless of what the user specifies.\n\nLet me also check if there are any other issues in the implementation. I need to understand the full context:\n\n1. The `_add_tokens` method overrides the parent's behavior by forcing all tokens to be special\n2. The EsmTokenizer has custom `_id_to_token` and `_token_to_id` dictionaries\n3. The EsmTokenizer also has a custom `get_vocab()` method\n\nNow, let me also check if the EsmTokenizer is updating its internal dictionaries when tokens are added. Looking at the code:\n- `_id_to_token` and `_token_to_id` are initialized in `__init__` based on the loaded vocabulary\n- But when `_add_tokens` is called (which is called by `add_tokens`), these dictionaries are not updated\n\nLet me verify this by checking if there's any code in the parent class that updates these mappings.\n\nActually, looking at the base class implementation at line 479:\n```python\nis_special = token in self.all_special_tokens or special_tokens\n```\n\nThe issue is that when `special_tokens=True` is forced, the token is marked as special. But the real issue reported in the task is that \"the length of the tokenizer does not change after adding the new token\". Let me check the `get_vocab_size` method in the EsmTokenizer.\n\nLooking at the EsmTokenizer code:\n\n\nThe `get_vocab_size()` method returns `len(self._id_to_token)`, which is based on `self.all_tokens` loaded from the file. Now I see the full picture:\n\n1. The `__len__` method in PreTrainedTokenizer returns `len(set(self.get_vocab().keys()))`\n2. The EsmTokenizer overrides `get_vocab()` to return `{token: i for i, token in enumerate(self.all_tokens)}`\n3. The problem is that `get_vocab()` only returns the base vocabulary from `self.all_tokens` and DOES NOT include the added tokens tracked by the parent class\n\nSo the issues are:\n1. `_add_tokens` is {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_018j1mQNV4mQKgxZRtF6P4oP","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Perfect! Now I can see the issues clearly:1. Line 162: The `_add_tokens` method is forcing `special_tokens=True` regardless of the user's input\n2. Line 98: The `get_vocab()` method only returns the base tokens from `self.all_tokens`, not including added tokens\n3. Line 95: The `get_vocab_size()` method only counts `self._id_to_token`, which doesn't include added tokens\n\nThe `__len__` method in the parent class calls `get_vocab()`, so if `get_vocab()` doesn't include added tokens, the length won't increase.\n\nAlso, the `_id_to_token` and `_token_to_id` dictionaries are never updated when tokens are added. The parent class manages added tokens separately using `_added_tokens_decoder` and `_added_tokens_encoder`.\n\nThe fixes needed are:1. Remove the override of `_add_tokens` to allow the parent class behavior or change it to respect the `special_tokens` parameter\n2. Update `get_vocab()` to include added tokens\n3. Update `get_vocab_size()` to include added tokens\n4. Update `_id_to_token` and `_token_to_id` when tokens are added\n\nLet me check what the parent class's `_add_tokens` does and what properties/methods it updates to understand what I need to Tokens were automatically classified as special tokens despite specifying `special_tokens=False`\n2. The tokenizer's length did not increase after adding new tokens\n3. Added tokens were not included in the vocabulary\n\n## Root Causes\nThe EsmTokenizer class had three critical issues:\n\n### Issue 1: `_add_tokens` Method (Line 161-162)\n**Before:**\n```python\ndef _add_tokens(self, new_tokens: Union[List[str], List[AddedToken]], special_tokens: bool = False) -> int:\n    return super()._add_tokens(new_tokens, special_tokens=True)\n```\nThe method was hardcoding `special_tokens=True` regardless of user input, forcing all new tokens to be marked as special.\n\n### Issue 2: `get_vocab` Method (Line 97-98)\n**Before:**\n```python\ndef get_vocab(self):\n    return {token: i for i, token in enumerate(self.all_tokens)}\n```\nThe method only returned base tokens and excluded added tokens, causing `__len__` (which calls `get_vocab()`) to not count added tokens.\n\n### Issue 3: `get_vocab_size` Method (Line 94-95)\n**Before:**\n```python\ndef get_vocab_size(self, with_added_tokens=False):\n    return len(self._id_to_token)\n```\nThe method ignored the `with_added_tokens` parameter and always returned the same value.\n\n## Solution\n\n### Fix 1: Updated `_add_tokens` Method (Line 166-172)\n```python\ndef _add_tokens(self, new_tokens: Union[List[str], List[AddedToken]], special_tokens: bool = False) -> int:\n    added = super()._add_tokens(new_tokens, special_tokens=special_tokens)\n    # Update the internal mappings to include added tokens\n    for token_index, token in self._added_tokens_decoder.items():\n        self._id_to_token[token_index] = str(token)\n        self._token_to_id[str(token)] = token_index\n    return added\n```\n\n**Changes:**\n- Now respects the `special_tokens` parameter and passes it to parent class\n- Updates internal `_id_to_token` and `_token_to_id` dictionaries to track added tokens\n- Ensures tokens added with `special_tokens=False` are not marked as special\n\n### Fix 2: Updated `get_vocab` Method (Line 100-103)\n```python\ndef get_vocab(self):\n    vocab = {token: i for i, token in enumerate(self.all_tokens)}\n    vocab.update(self._added_tokens_encoder)\n    return vocab\n```\n\n**Changes:**\n- Merges base vocabulary with added tokens from parent class's `_added_tokens_encoder`\n- Ensures `__len__` counts all tokens (including added ones)\n\n### Fix 3: Updated `get_vocab_size` Method (Line 94-98)\n```python\ndef get_vocab_size(self, with_added_tokens=False):\n    base_size = len(self.all_tokens)\n    if with_added_tokens:\n        base_size += len(self._added_tokens_encoder)\n    return base_size\n```\n\n**Changes:**\n- Now respects the `with_added_tokens` parameter\n- Returns base vocabulary size when `with_added_tokens=False` (default)\n- Returns base + added tokens count when `with_added_tokens=True`\n\n## Expected Behavior After Fix\n\n```python\nmodel_checkpoint = \"facebook/esm2_t6_8M_UR50D\"\nmodel = AutoModelForMaskedLM.from_pretrained(model_checkpoint)\ntokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_checkpoint)\n\n# Before fix:\n# num_added_toks = tokenizer.add_tokens(['J'], special_tokens=False)\n# len(tokenizer) would not increase\n# 'J' would be in additional_special_tokens\n\n# After fix:\nnum_added_toks = tokenizer.add_tokens(['J'], special_tokens=False)\nprint(f\"Added tokens: {num_added_toks}\")  #
